LLM supporting:  lubag disease, also known as x - linked dystonia - parkinsonism, is a neurological disorder characterized by the presence of both dystonia and parkinsonian features. the primary symptoms include involuntary movements ( dystonia ) and tremors, rigidity, and bradykinesia ( parkinsonian features ). this condition is caused by mutations in the dyt1 gene, which encodes the protein torsin a. torsin a is involved in the regulation of neurotransmitter release and is thought to play a role in the development and function of the basal ganglia, a group of structures that are crucial for the control of movement. the disease is typically inherited in an x - linked recessive pattern, meaning that it affects males more severely than females. in males, the mutation leads to the production of abnormal torsin a, which disrupts the normal functioning of the basal ganglia, leading to the characteristic motor symptoms. in females, the mutation may be present but is often associated with milder or no symptoms due to the presence of a second, normal x chromosome. the primary treatment for lubag disease includes medications such as anticholinergics, benzodiazepines, and dopaminergic agents, which can help manage the symptoms. deep brain stimulation ( dbs ) is also a therapeutic option that has been shown to be effective in reducing the severity of symptoms in some patients.

LLM supporting:  supporting sentence : multiple sclerosis ( ms ) is the leading cause of neurological disability in young adults, affecting some two million people worldwide. traditionally, ms has been considered a chronic, inflammatory disorder of the central white matter in which ensuing demyelination results in physical disability [ frohman em, racke mk, raine cs ( 2006 ) n engl j med 354 : 942 - 955 ]. more recently, ms has become increasingly viewed as a neurodegenerative disorder in which neuronal loss, axonal injury, and atrophy of the cns lead to permanent neurological and clinical disability. although axonal pathology and loss in ms has been recognized for > 100 years, very little is known about the underlying molecular mechanisms. progressive axonal loss in ms may stem from a cascade of ionic imbalances initiated by inflammation, leading to mitochondrial dysfunction and energetic deficits that result in mitochondrial and cellular ca2 + overload. in a murine disease model, experimental autoimmune encephalomyelitis ( eae ) mice lacking cyclophilin d ( cypd ), a key regulator of the mitochondrial permeability transition pore ( ptp ), developed eae, but unlike wt mice, they partially recovered. examination of the spinal cords of cypd - knockout mice revealed a striking preservation of axons, despite a similar extent of inflammation. furthermore, neurons prepared from cypd - knockout animals were resistant to reactive oxygen and nitrogen species thought to mediate axonal damage in eae and ms, and brain mitochondria lacking cypd sequestered substantially higher levels of ca2 +. our results directly implicate pathological activation of the mitochondrial ptp in the axonal damage occurring during ms and identify cypd, as well as the ptp, as a potential target for ms neuroprotective therapies.

=== idx: 957 ===
Gold supporting: transcription - coupled repair ( tcr ) is generally observed as more rapid or more efficient removal of certain types of dna damage from the transcribed strands of expressed genes compared with the nontranscribed strands.
LLM supporting:  transcription - coupled repair ( tcr ) primarily targets the non - template ( or sense ) strand of dna. this is because tcr is initiated in response to damage that is encountered by the rna polymerase during transcription, and the non - template strand is the one being transcribed. the process involves the removal of the transcribed rna polymerase and the recruitment of the nucleotide excision repair ( ner ) machinery, which then excises the damaged segment of the non - template dna strand. this allows for the correction of the dna damage and the resumption of transcription. however, the exact mechanisms and the specific types of dna damage that tcr can handle, such as oxidative lesions, are still under investigation.
